Footage continued to show [foreign Shia militias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Involvement_of_foreign_militias_in_the_suppression_of_protests_in_Iran), namely the Iraqi [Popular Mobilization Forces](45671374.md) and the Afghan [Liwa Fatemiyoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liwa_Fatemiyoun), parading through streets in Iran.[^cite_note-358][^cite_note-359]

On 5 April, Trump stated that the Iranian people were "living in fear" and were "afraid that \[the United States would] leave them in the middle of [the war](82533582.md)".[^cite_note-360]

After the [8 April ceasefire](82888027.md), some Iranians stated they feel a sense of abandonment and despair, while some described the truce as a missed opportunity for political change that would allow the authorities to intensify domestic repression.[^cite_note-361] The [internet blackout](82063825.md#Renewed_blackout) continued after the 8 April ceasefire, passing the 1,000-hour mark by 11 April.[^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363] On 14 April, armed security forces continued to be deployed in the streets.[^cite_note-364]

#### Late April tiered Internet protests

In late April, during the [internet blackout](82063825.md), officials associated with president [Masoud Pezeshkian](2650383.md) and several Iranian citizens' associations and individuals protested against the imposition by IRGC-aligned authorities of [tiered Internet access](82063825.md#Tiered_Internet_access) ([Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language): *Internet Tabaghati*[^cite_note-IranWire_students_creators-365]).[^cite_note-Rudaw_exposes_rifts-366][^cite_note-IranIntl_IR_designers_reject-367]

On 22 April, Mehdi Tabatabaei, a spokesperson for Pezeshkian, stated that the tiered internet was "entirely inconsistent and in conflict with the policies ... and views of the president". [Mohammad Reza Aref](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Reza_Aref), one of the vice presidents of Iran, stated on the same day that "equal and non-discriminatory access to the internet must be provided for all segments of society". [Minister of Information and Communications Technology of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Information_and_Communications_Technology_of_Iran) [Sattar Hashemi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sattar_Hashemi) stated that "fair access to the internet is the right of all people, ... even from a security perspective, access should not be restricted through classification or whitelist systems."[^cite_note-Rudaw_exposes_rifts-366]

On 26 and 27 April, an organisation of Iranian nurses and another Iranian citizens' association, the Graphic Designers Society, refused the authorities' proposals that the two organisations obtain access for their members to an "Internet Pro" (tiered Internet) exception to the internet blackout, arguing that it would be unfair until all citizens had regular Internet access. The Graphics Designers Society stated that "Free and affordable [access to the internet is the right of all people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_to_Internet_access), and \[the society] expect\[s] authorities to restore this right equally for everyone to prevent further losses for graphic designers."[^cite_note-IranIntl_IR_designers_reject-367] A group of [Tehran University of Medical Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehran_University_of_Medical_Sciences) students published a statement declaring equal access to the Internet to be part of the [rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fundamental_human_rights) to [education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_to_education) and [science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_to_science_and_culture), stating that unequal access would constitute [discrimination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Discrimination), and refusing to use the tiered Internet access. The Internet firm Andropay refused to use the tiered Internet access, stating that tiered access would create a "gap between content creators and their audience".[^cite_note-IranWire_students_creators-365] Criticisms of the tiered access within Iran included that access for sellers was pointless if buyers didn't have access. On 30 April, Tehran Province Journalists' Association criticised the tiered access as bad for business, "against citizens' legal rights" and "unethical", called for it to be "removed from the government's agenda", and stated that "Access to free, high-quality and universal internet is not a luxury but a public right, and governments are responsible for ensuring it." According to [Iran International](58555280.md), a common reason cited by Iranians for refusal to participate in the tiered access was an objection to unequal Internet access.[^cite_note-IranIntl_denounce_tiered-368]

The [Reformists Front](50063989.md), an alliance of Iranian political parties, stated its objections to tiered Internet access on 30 April, asserted that equal access to the Internet beyond Iran is a citizens' right, and called for "people's access to stable, free and secure internetaccess \[to] be restored immediately".[^cite_note-IranIntl_Trump_IR_under_blocked-369]

### May/June student protests

In late May and early June, high school students protested in 20 provinces, objecting to repeated changes to exam and university entrance rules and educational conditions. In early June, students protested in [Khorramabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khorramabad), calling for transparency in exam conditions, and in [Arak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arak,_Iran) and [Isfahan](223149.md), opposing "in-person exams and poor educational conditions". Some students were injured in [Yazd](451385.md). At least one person was arrested in [Qom](638742.md). A protest in Tehran outside the [Ministry of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Education_\(Iran\)) opposed the way that 11th-grade grades were used in university entrance exams. Slogans used in protests on 6 June included "Incompetent official we don't want you" in [Shahr-e Kord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahr-e_Kord) and "Justice, education, our undeniable right" in Tehran in front of the [Supreme Council of the Cultural Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Council_of_the_Cultural_Revolution).[^cite_note-IranIntl_widen_final_exams-370] The protests grew in the first week of June. University students complained after 75 were hospitalised after eating subsidized meals at [Shahid Sadoughi University of Medical Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahid_Sadoughi_University_of_Medical_Sciences). Several students were expelled, suspended, or banned from entering Shahid Sadoughi University, [Sharif University of Technology](453155.md) and [Kermanshah University of Medical Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kermanshah_University_of_Medical_Sciences) for online and protest-related activities.[^cite_note-JPost_crackdown_new_stud_protests-371] The Coordination Council of Iranian Educators' Unions stated that the exam rules created "psychological pressure and widening inequality within the education system".[^cite_note-Rudaw_stud_edu_policies-372]

## Methods



The [Lion and Sun flag](82020140.md) has become a widely used symbol of opposition to the Islamic Republic. Although its display inside Iran is strictly banned, protesters have increasingly begun waving it despite the serious risks involved.

[^cite_note-373]

[^cite_note-Iran_International-2026b-374]

[^cite_note-375]

### Protesters

#### National strikes

Nationwide strikes were conducted by businesses, stores, cafes, and workers as well as by online shops and social media influencers.[^cite_note-376][^cite_note-377][^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379][^cite_note-380]

#### Demonstrations

Protests took the form of street demonstrations, chanting slogans, [car honking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vehicle_horn),[^cite_note-radiozamaneh20260111-381][^cite_note-382] lighting fires,[^cite_note-radiozamaneh20260111-381] and removing surveillance cameras.[^cite_note-383][^cite_note-384] To avoid being identified and later arrested, many protesters wore masks and dark clothes, reminiscent of *[V for Vendetta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V_for_Vendetta_\(film\))*.[^cite_note-auto14-230] In some cases, when the street lights were turned off by the authorities, the protesters set off [fireworks](59493.md) and lit the area using their [smartphones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smartphone).[^cite_note-radiozamaneh20260111-381][^cite_note-\:34-227][^cite_note-auto13-228]

#### Slogans and symbols

Main article: [Political slogans against the Islamic Republic of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_slogans_against_the_Islamic_Republic_of_Iran)

During the protests, several notable slogans were chanted by demonstrators, reflecting anti-government sentiments, calls for the restoration of the monarchy, and unity among protesters. These slogans were frequently documented in videos and reports by Persian-language media outlets such as *[Manoto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manoto)* and *[Iran International](58555280.md)*. Many drew on historical references to the [Pahlavi dynasty](338883.md), while others directly targeted Supreme Leader [Ali Khamenei](385653.md) or the Islamic Republic's leadership. Symbols associated with the protests included the [Lion and Sun flag](82020140.md), which was waved in several demonstrations as a sign of monarchist aspirations and opposition to the government.[^cite_note-Iran_International-2026b-374][^cite_note-Iran_International-2025d-72][^cite_note-385] On 9 January 2026, [X](9988187.md) changed the Iran [flag emoji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_emoji) from the Islamic Republic flag to the [modern design](82020140.md#Modern_design) of the Lion and Sun flag.[^cite_note-Iran_International-2026g-218]

- "[Death to the Dictator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_to_the_Dictator)" ([Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language): مرگ بر دیکتاتور, [romanised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Persian): *Marg bar Diktâtor*) — a general anti-authoritarian chant targeting the government's leadership, reported in [Tehran](57654.md), [Isfahan](223149.md), [Kermanshah](1489244.md), [Malard](17437690.md), [Nahavand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahavand), [Noorabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noorabad,_Fasa), and [Karaj](978698.md).

  [^cite_note-392]

- "[Death to Khamenei](79312660.md)" ([Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language): مرگ بر خامنه‌ای, [romanised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Persian): *Marg bar Khâmene'i*) — direct call against former supreme leader Ali Khamenei and current supreme leader Mojtaba Khamenei, heard in Isfahan, [Farsan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farsan), [Arak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arak,_Iran), and other protest sites. Previously used during the [2021–2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021%E2%80%932022_Iranian_protests) and [2022–2023 protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahsa_Amini_protests).

  [^cite_note-397]

- "Death to Mojtaba" ([Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language): مرگ بر مجتبی, [romanised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Persian): *Marg bar Mojtabâ*) — direct call against supreme leader Mojtaba Khamenei, heard from the windows of homes in Tehran.

  [^cite_note-protester2026-322]

- "[Javid Shah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javid_Shah)" ([Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language): جاوید شاه, [romanised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Persian): *Jâvid Shâh*, [lit.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literal_translation) 'Long live the [Shah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shah)') — repeated chants calling for the return of the [monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchism_in_Iran), heard in [Hamadan](43610555.md), [Arak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arak,_Iran), [Nahavand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahavand), [Dehloran](33014014.md), and many other cities during nighttime protests.

  [^cite_note-404]

- "[This is the final battle, Pahlavi will return](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/This_is_the_final_battle,_Pahlavi_will_return)" ([Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language): این آخرین نبرده، پهلوی برمی‌گرده, [romanised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Persian): *In âkharin nabarde, Pahlavi barmigarde*) — a phrase conveying determination for regime change and the return of the [Pahlavi family](338883.md), chanted in Arak, [Rasht](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rasht), [Khorramabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khorramabad), Isfahan, Nahavand, and [Dorud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dorud).

  [^cite_note-408]

- "[Neither Gaza nor Lebanon, My Life for Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neither_Gaza_nor_Lebanon,_My_Life_for_Iran)" ([Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language): نه غزه نه لبنان، جانم فدای ایران, [romanised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Persian): *Na Qazze na Lobnan, jânam fadâ-ye Irân*) — a chant that expresses the opposition of some Iranians to the Islamic Republic's military, financial, and political support for [Palestinian militant groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_militant_groups), neglecting the needs and interests of Iran itself.

  [^cite_note-BBC_News-2025-108]

  [^cite_note-Weiniger-2026-409]

- "[Reza Shah, bless your soul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reza_Shah,_bless_your_soul)" ([Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language): رضا شاه، روحت شاد, [romanised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Persian): *Rezâ Shâh, ruhat shâd*) — a slogan honouring Reza Shah Pahlavi, founder of the Pahlavi dynasty, chanted in Hamadan, Tehran, Isfahan, Kermanshah, and other locations.

  [^cite_note-410]

  [^cite_note-Iran_International-2025g-394]

- "Don't be afraid, don't be afraid, we are all together" ([Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language): نترسید، نترسید، ما همه با هم هستیم, [romanised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Persian): *Natarsid, natarsid, mâ hame bâ ham hastim*) — a chant promoting unity and courage among protesters, chanted during nighttime gatherings in Isfahan.

  [^cite_note-411]

  [^cite_note-Iran_International-2025f-407]

- "Dishonourable, dishonourable" ([Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language): بی‌شرف، بی‌شرف, [romanised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Persian): *Bi-sharaf, bi-sharaf*) — directed at security forces or government officials.

  [^cite_note-412]

- "Freedom, freedom, freedom" ([Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language): آزادی، آزادی، آزادی, [romanised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Persian): *Āzādi, āzādi, āzādi*).

  [^cite_note-413]

  [^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-396]

- "The [Shah](613947.md) is coming home, [Zahhak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zahhak) is overthrown" ([Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language): شاه می‌یاد به خونه، ضحاک سرنگونه, [romanised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Persian): *Shâh mi-yâd be khune, Zahâk sarnegune*) — referencing [Persian mythology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_mythology) ([Zahhak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zahhak) as a tyrant) to symbolise the overthrow of the current government and return of the Shah.

- "Death to the oppressor, whether [Shah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shah) or [Rahbar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rahbar)" ([Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language): مرگ بر ستمگر، چه شاه باشد چه رهبر, [romanised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Persian): *Marg bar setamgar, che shah bashad che rahbar*) — a general anti-Islamic Republic and anti-monarchy chant mainly by expressed by supporters of the [National Council of Resistance of Iran](1181356.md) (NCRI).

  [^cite_note-Jones-2026-17]

  [^cite_note-Karim-2025-18]

#### Organisation

As of 7 January, *HRANA* viewed the protests as being networked.[^cite_note-Human_Rights_Activists_in_Iran_News_Agency-2026d-193] The [Associated Press](18935732.md) viewed the first steps of protests as "broadly leaderless" before 8 January, and described Reza Pahlavi's influence after the call for demonstration on 8 January as unclear; however, when the time clocked at 8 p.m., chanting broke out across Tehran, with crowds shouting anti-government slogans and expressing support for the shah's return.[^cite_note-414] *[IranWire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IranWire)* viewed [Generation Z](4015968.md) Iranians as "one of the most visible and active groups" in the 2025–2026 protests, whose political views were strongly impacted by the [Mahsa Amini protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahsa_Amini_protests) of 2022–2023.[^cite_note-415]

## Government suppression methods

### Internet blackouts

Main article: [2026 Internet blackout in Iran](82063825.md)

On 8 January 2026, the government imposed significant restrictions on telephone and internet access to limit communication and the dissemination of information. Unlike the [Twelve-Day War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War), there has not been an official internet shut down nationwide. However, connectivity was heavily disrupted in cities experiencing active demonstrations, making it difficult for citizens to send messages, share media, or organise further protests. These measures were widely seen as part of the authorities' efforts to suppress dissent and control the narrative around the unrest.[^cite_note-416]

The next day, on 9 January, it was reported by multiple media outlets that Iran, in a largely unprecedented measure, had activated military-grade [jammers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_jamming) to disrupt civilian [Starlink](45111627.md) signals.[^cite_note-Doffman-236][^cite_note-iranwire.com-417][^cite_note-418] Initially, only 30 percent of the media traffic was affected but it rose to 80 percent within several hours.[^cite_note-Doffman-236][^cite_note-iranwire.com-417] However, from the morning after the blackout began, Islamic Republic authorities issued a "white list" which allowed government affiliated institutions and accounts limited access to the internet, included were government aligned media and [Telegram](41501305.md) channels, as well as some universities.[^cite_note-iranwire.com-417] [Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes) quoted [VPN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virtual_private_network) expert Simon Migliano[^cite_note-419] as saying that "Iran's current nationwide blackout is a blunt instrument intended to crush dissent."[^cite_note-Doffman-236] Migliano also addressed the cost of the internet shutdown, saying "this 'kill switch' approach comes at a staggering price, draining \$1.56 million from Iran's economy every single hour the internet is down."[^cite_note-Doffman-236] By 11 January, Iran shut down the Starlink internet for the first time.[^cite_note-Doffman-236]

Thereafter, security forces started door-to-door operations, seizing [satellite dishes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satellite_dish) amid the blackout to block any external access.[^cite_note-420] On 14 January, reports emerged that the government was finalising their internet kill switch project, which was intended to cut the nation completely off from the global internet for extended periods; this plan was being coordinated with [Huawei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huawei) and [China](5405.md).[^cite_note-\:4-421] By 16 January, after more than 200 hours of the blackout, [NetBlocks](59736785.md) reported a very slight internet connectivity uptick at 2%.[^cite_note-uptick-276][^cite_note-uptick2-277] Reports also indicated that the government was planning on permanently cutting internet access, with only vetted individuals being permitted filtered and [censored](4297092.md) access.[^cite_note-422] On 17 January, it was reported that [CCTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CCTV) footage was being confiscated, and checkpoints were set up to detain citizens found with protest images on their phones.[^cite_note-checkpoints-279] On 18 January, [Meta](62420226.md) began hiding the [Instagram](31591547.md) followers of Iranian residents after reports indicated that Iranian security bodies were extracting large volumes of user data.[^cite_note-423] After [targeting Iranians abroad with cybersecurity threats and phishing scams](82164645.md#Violence_towards_diaspora_protesters), it was reported on 19 January that the Iran-linked ["Handala Hack" group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Handala_\(hacker_group\)) was routing attacks via Starlink.[^cite_note-424][^cite_note-425]

On 20 January, it was reported by an investigative watchdog that pro-government editors were active during the crackdown across [Wikipedia](5043734.md) and other services in [deleting content and sanitising articles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical_negationism) related to the [Islamic Republic's human rights](3156692.md) record.[^cite_note-426] The same day, it was also reported that Iranian authorities claimed they had cut off 40,000 Starlink satellite internet connections during the internet blackout.[^cite_note-427][^cite_note-428] On 21 January, it was reported that the Islamic Republic was working on a state-run [intranet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intranet) amid the ongoing blackout, similar to that of [North Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_in_North_Korea).[^cite_note-429] On 23 January, NetBlocks reported the blackout was entering its third week while the Iranian government was attempting to generate false traffic in order to manufacture the narrative of a wider restoration and normalcy.[^cite_note-430] During the blackout, Iranian diaspora internet users ran applications to share part of their bandwidth in an attempt to help users inside Iran circumvent the blackout.[^cite_note-psiphon-431] Separately, an independent research group stated the Islamic Republic had coordinated a large social media influence operation aimed at shaping global narratives.[^cite_note-social-media-influence-operation-432] *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* also reported the Islamic Republic used Chinese technology during the internet blackout and suppression of protests, such as facial recognition and surveillance tools.[^cite_note-433]

### Recruitment of foreign militias

The presence of [state-sponsored foreign Shia militias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Involvement_of_foreign_militias_in_the_suppression_of_protests_in_Iran), namely the Iraqi [Popular Mobilisation Forces](45671374.md), Arabic-speaking mercenaries, Lebanese [Hezbollah](13919.md), the Pakistani [Liwa Zainabiyoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liwa_Zainabiyoun), and the Afghan [Liwa Fatemiyoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liwa_Fatemiyoun), in suppressing protests was reported.[^cite_note-434][^cite_note-435][^cite_note-Iran_International-436][^cite_note-437] *[Iran International](58555280.md)* reported that on 2 January 2026, Iraqi militias affiliated with the Iranian government recruited forces to assist Iranian security forces in suppressing protests in Iran.[^cite_note-Iran_International-436] On 6 January 2026, it was reported that approximately 800 members of Iraqi Shia militia groups, including [Kata'ib Hezbollah](23626646.md), [Harakat al-Nujaba](48027962.md), [Sayyid al-Shuhada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kata'ib_Sayyid_ul-Shuhada), and the [Badr Organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Badr_Organization) had been sent to Iran.[^cite_note-Iran_International-436] The troops were reportedly transported through the border crossings of [Shalamcheh](8247289.md), [Chazabeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chazabeh), and [Khosravi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khosravi,_Kermanshah), officially under the cover of a "pilgrimage to the holy sites of [Imam Reza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imam_Reza) in [Mashhad](406512.md)", while in practice they were gathered at a base in [Ahvaz](599079.md) before being dispatched to various regions to assist in suppressing protests.[^cite_note-Iran_International-436]

Roni Insaz, a former member of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps, in his interview with *The Jerusalem Post*, explained "It's very difficult for Iranian forces to kill Iranians, because they are the same people," and added that loyal militias from Syria, Lebanon, Afghanistan, and Pakistan have participated for firing at the protestors.[^cite_note-l558-438] According to *Iran International*, "The reason behind this move by the Islamic Republic could be its concern that the Iranian police might not follow orders to attack unarmed, ordinary people, or simply because its forces are insufficient to stop protests in more than 100 cities".[^cite_note-439]

On 9 January 2026 the United States warned Iran against using foreign militias to crush protests.[^cite_note-440] According to [The Media Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Media_Line), Iraqi Shiite militia members were recruited to help suppress Iranian protesters, receiving \$600 each. By 11 January, more than 60 buses, each carrying about 50 people, had crossed the Iraq‑Iran border.[^cite_note-441] On 14 January, a source told [IHRNGO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Human_Rights) that the security forces in the Kurdish regions of Iran during the killings did not speak [Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language), while in Karaj, an eyewitness said the forces spoke [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language) and took [selfies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selfie) with the bodies.[^cite_note-\:7-442] On 15 January, an Iraqi source stated to CNN that "nearly 5,000" fighters from Iraqi militias had crossed into Iran over the preceding weeks.[^cite_note-5000-iraq-47]

### Internal propaganda and coercion

The Iranian government has been accused of using footage of protesters' bodies in morgues to demoralise future protests.[^cite_note-ABC_News-2026-443] Families trying to receive the bodies of their loved ones have oftentimes been forced to pay compensation for the bullets that killed their relatives.[^cite_note-ABC_News-2026-443]\[^cite_note-[www.iranintl.com-444](http://www.iranintl.com-444/)] Reports stated that security forces and Revolutionary Guard members raided and intimidated the families of protesters who were killed, imposed restrictions on the retrieval and burial of bodies, and warned that families would be charged fees.\[^cite_note-[www.iranintl.com-444](http://www.iranintl.com-444/)] There have been reports that families were unable to locate the remains of their relatives after [authorities secretly buried them in locations far from where the deaths occurred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abduction_of_bodies_by_the_Islamic_Republic_of_Iran).[^cite_note-ABC_News-2026-443][^cite_note-iran-intl-burials-445] Reports have also indicated that the authorities retained the remains until families consented to official accounts describing the deceased as aligned with the government and [Basij](2892082.md) rather than as protesters.[^cite_note-ABC_News-2026-443][^cite_note-446] Likewise, images and videos from the pro-government rallies were reported to have been [altered](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Video_manipulation).\[^cite_note-[www.iranintl.com-2026b-243](http://www.iranintl.com-2026b-243/)][^cite_note-frane24-ai-244]\[^cite_note-\:4-421] *[HRANA](39793882.md)* also reported cases of [forced confessions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forced_confessions) being broadcast.[^cite_note-hrana-jan18-447] Furthermore, doctors and healthcare workers were reported to have been arrested or disappeared for providing medical care to protesters or documenting their injuries.[^cite_note-doctors-disappeared-448][^cite_note-449][^cite_note-450] The Islamic Republic also pressured families of detainees to attend pro-government rallies planned for 22 Bahman (11 February), marking the [anniversary of the 1979 Islamic Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anniversary_of_the_Iranian_Revolution), telling them that only if they complied and attended would their detained relatives be spared execution or have their sentences reduced.[^cite_note-pro-gov-rally-coercion-451]

### Direct order for live fire on protesters and killing of hospitalised protesters

Sources close to Iran's Supreme National Security Council and the presidential office report that the killing of protesters was carried out on the direct order of Supreme Leader Ali Khamenei, with full approval from senior state officials. The council allegedly authorised live fire, which was executed mainly by the IRGC in what is described as a deliberate, organised operation exceptional in scale and intensity.\[^cite_note-[www.iranintl.com-2026a-452](http://www.iranintl.com-2026a-452/)]

On 13 January, *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* reported that Islamic Republic security forces were documented using shotguns and rifles with live ammunition,[^cite_note-\:5-453] as well as heavy [DShK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DShK) machine guns against protesters,[^cite_note-\:7-442] with a Tehran doctor stating that security forces were "shooting to kill".[^cite_note-\:5-453] Further reports also indicated the security forces used snipers and knives to attack protesters.[^cite_note-454][^cite_note-455][^cite_note-456][^cite_note-457] A spokesperson from the Abdorrahman Boroumand Centre for Human Rights cited evidence that even when using "less lethal" weapons, security forces were deliberately shooting at the heads, eyes, genitals and vital organs of the protesters, so as to terrorise protesters by mutilating them and causing them permanent disability,[^cite_note-\:5-453] reusing [the tactic employed in the 2022 Mahsa Amini protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eye_injuries_during_2022_Iranian_protests).[^cite_note-\:5-453] At least one young girl had been shot in the [pelvic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pelvis) area and was in critical condition.[^cite_note-\:5-453] A medic in Tehran reported that there were "direct shots to the heads of the young people, to their hearts as well."[^cite_note-458]

Additionally, multiple testimonies have revealed Iranian security forces raiding hospitals to arrest,[^cite_note-459] and in many cases execute, hospitalised protesters.[^cite_note-\:6-460][^cite_note-\:7-442] On 4 January, according to Namdar Baghaei Yazdi, vice president of the Iranian Medical Society UK, security forces in full riot gear stormed Imam Khomeini Hospital in Ilam, attacked medical personnel with tear gas and shotgun pellets, and arrested injured protesters,[^cite_note-\:9-461] with another similar assault being carried out by security forces on 6 January in Sina Hospital in Tehran.[^cite_note-\:9-461] Yazdi was quoted as saying "Hospitals are no longer sacred in Iran, and we are very concerned for our medical colleagues there who are already at risk from the regime."[^cite_note-\:9-461] A doctor from southern Iran reported that security forces had "finished off" protesters who had been hospitalised at the time,[^cite_note-\:7-442][^cite_note-\:6-460] further stating "they killed many, arrested many, and many are on the run. The situation is very bad."[^cite_note-\:6-460] According to The Times, another doctor from Tehran stated that security forces had "gone into hospitals and forcibly taken the corpses of protesters with them", and some of the wounded protesters treat their injuries at home and avoid being admitted to the hospital out of fear of being arrested.[^cite_note-462] [Iran Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Human_Rights) reported that in [Rasht](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rasht), security forces [surrounded and trapped protesters inside the Rasht Bazaar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Rasht_massacre), set it on fire, and killed people attempting to surrender or escape, while also "finishing off" wounded survivors in the streets and in hospitals.[^cite_note-\:7-442]

### Chemical substances

On 17 January, reports indicated the Iranian government may have used [toxic chemical substances](27179600.md) against protesters.[^cite_note-chemical1-463][^cite_note-464][^cite_note-chemical2-465] Footage showed security forces atop vehicles wearing hazmat suits and masks designed for hazardous chemical materials.[^cite_note-chemical1-463] Some victims reportedly died several days after exposure rather than immediately.[^cite_note-chemical1-463][^cite_note-chemical2-465] On 18 January, it was reported that the detained protesters were being injected with unknown substances while in custody.[^cite_note-chemical-injections-466] On 23 January, chemical gas was reported to have been used on the crowds of protesters and escape routes, causing severe breathing problems, burning pain of the eyes, skin and lungs, vomiting blood, and sudden weakness and loss of movement.[^cite_note-467] On 1 February, it was reported that the unknown substances that were being forcibly injected into the detainees had been linked to multiple deaths in custody.[^cite_note-injection-death-468]

### Concealment of deaths through mass graves and burials

On 27 January, *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* reported that the Islamic Republic was concealing protest deaths through [mass graves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_graves_in_Iran) and burials.[^cite_note-guardian-mass-burials-51][^cite_note-iran-intl-mass-burials-469][^cite_note-iran-intl-burials-445]

### Persecution and executions

On 5 January 2026, Gholamhossein Mohseni Ejei, head of the judiciary in the Islamic Republic, stated that there would be no leniency for "rioters" despite the right to demonstrate,[^cite_note-\:10-470][^cite_note-\:11-471] and the judiciary's [Mizan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mizan_News_Agency) news agency quoted him saying "I instruct the attorney general and prosecutors across the country to act in accordance with the law and with resolve against the rioters and those who support them (...) and to show no leniency or indulgence",[^cite_note-\:10-470][^cite_note-\:11-471] and stressing that the penalty would be "decisive" and "maximum".[^cite_note-auto1-472] Regarding the rapid trials and executions or protesters, Iran state television shared a video in which Mohseni-Ejei said "If we want to do a job, we should do it now. If we want to do something, we have to do it quickly, if it becomes late, two months, three months later, it doesn't have the same effect. If we want to do something, we have to do that fast."[^cite_note-auto-473][^cite_note-474] On 10 January, Supreme Leader Ali Khamenei said that the demands of protesters in the country are "completely fair", but "rioters" should "be put in their place."[^cite_note-\:11-471] On 13 January, in a televised statement from the office of the Tehran prosecutor, the office declared that an undeclared number of protesters would be charged with "[moharebeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moharebeh)", or "waging war against God", an offence [punished by death in Iran](5170842.md), and used extensively in the past by the [Iranian judiciary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_judicial_system_of_Iran).[^cite_note-475] According to the Human Rights Activists News Agency, as of 14 January 2026, over 18,400 people had been arrested.[^cite_note-auto-473] By 18 January, *[HRANA](39793882.md)* reported that 24,669 protesters had been detained.[^cite_note-hrana-jan18-447]

According to Iran International, on 10 January 2026 the "One Word" lawyers' network, citing the internet shutdown isolating protesters from the outside world, called on the international community and Iranian judges to prevent the show trials and extrajudicial executions of protesters following the orders of Ali Khamenei and senior judicial officials.[^cite_note-\:12-476] In its statement, the network detailed new orders from Khamenei instructing security forces "to deal harshly with protesters in recent gatherings" as well as separate statements from the Head of the Judiciary, the Attorney General of the country, and the Tehran Prosecutor calling for "extraordinary, out-of-order proceedings and the imposition of the most severe punishments in the cases of detained protesters."[^cite_note-\:12-476]

On 8 February 2026, chief judiciary, [Gholamhossein Mohseni Ejei](3002270.md) stated that detained individuals who had participated in the protests would be removed from the list of pardons commonly issued on the [anniversary of the Iranian Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anniversary_of_the_Iranian_Revolution).[^cite_note-477]

Executions of individuals allegedly involved in the protests continued during the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md). *The Guardian* reported on 2 March 2026 that the Iranian government has persisted in persecuting political prisoners and protest detainees, with hundreds reportedly executed or at risk of execution. Some people experience prolonged solitary confinement, torture, mock executions, and forced confessions, without access to lawyers. Executions during this period included those convicted of *moharebeh*, armed rebellion, or membership in opposition groups, with specific cases reported in March 2026. Families have frequently received little or contradictory information about their relatives' whereabouts. Meanwhile human rights groups note that the ongoing war has overshadowed these abuses even as the regime carries out death sentences and other severe punishments.[^cite_note-478]

On 1 April 2026, Iran's judiciary executed Amirhossein Hatami, a detainee convicted in connection with a fire at the "185 Mahmoud Kaveh" Basij base in Tehran during the protests. Reports indicate that he and other detainees were allegedly pushed into the base by armed individuals, after which the fire occurred, placing them at immediate risk.[^cite_note-479]

#### Torture and sexual violence

On 18 January, *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* reported that a human rights group claimed detainees were subjected to torture and sexual assault while in custody.[^cite_note-sexual-assault-480] *[Iran International](58555280.md)* also reported detainees were being injected with unknown chemical substances.[^cite_note-chemical-injections-466] Reports from KHRN (a French-based human rights organisation) cited by Iran International, claimed that two protesters were abused while in detention during Iran's nationwide unrest in the city of [Kermanshah](1489244.md).[^cite_note-\:14-481] One of those detained was a minor. According to these accounts, security personnel subjected the detainees to sexualised physical mistreatment during their transfer, including beatings and the use of [batons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baton_\(law_enforcement\)) through their clothing and over their anal areas.[^cite_note-\:14-481] Reports also stated imprisoned protesters were being tortured and raped, while women's bodies were being returned to their families with missing wombs in order to hide the crimes.[^cite_note-wombs-482]

## Casualties

Main article: [2026 Iran massacres](82036668.md)



Body bags of victims of the massacres.

Reporting on casualties from the 2026 protests has varied widely, from several thousand to around 30,000 deaths,[^cite_note-Time_30k_local_health_officials-52][^cite_note-36,500iranint-80] with figures difficult to verify due to media restrictions and the prolonged internet shutdown. Iran's Supreme Leader Ali Khamenei said that "several thousand" people had been killed, while attributing the violence to protesters. Independent and foreign media, citing medical professionals, human rights groups, and leaked hospital data, have reported substantially higher figures. On 17 January, *[The Sunday Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Sunday_Times)* reported that a network of Iranian doctors estimated between 16,500 and 18,000 deaths and hundreds of thousands of injuries; one doctor quoted in the report described the killings as [genocide](12441.md).[^cite_note-SundayTimes17Jan-55] On 21 January, the Iranian government issued a death toll of 3,117 people, of which 2,427 were "civilians and security forces", according to [Associated Press](18935732.md), which described the Iranian government as having a track record of undercounting deaths during protests.[^cite_note-483]

### Casualties, arrests, executions, and injured protesters

[Human Rights Watch](46530.md) reported that the Iranian government has been carrying out arbitrary arrests of tens of thousands of protesters, and has subjected them to torture and mistreatments.[^cite_note-484][^cite_note-485]

#### 31 December

On 31 December 2025, during a protest in [Fuladshahr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuladshahr), Dariush Ansari Bakhtiariwand was shot with a [Kalashnikov rifle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalashnikov_rifle) by security forces. He died before reaching medical care.[^cite_note-486] While participating in a protest in [Kuhdasht](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuhdasht) on 31 December, Amirhesam Khodayarifard (reported to be 21,[^cite_note-basij-Kohdasht-154] or 22 years old)[^cite_note-Hengaw-2026b-153] [was shot dead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Amirhesam_Khodayarifard) with a bullet to the head by a plainclothes retired IRGC agent.[^cite_note-Aida_Ghajar-2026-487] Eyewitness testimony and video evidence showed that Khodayarifard was among the protesters.[^cite_note-Hengaw-2026b-153] Government media stated that protesters had been throwing rocks at security forces, and that Khodayarafid was killed after the rocks had been thrown.[^cite_note-488]

Governmental media, including [Mehr News Agency](2707394.md),[^cite_note-basij-Kohdasht-154] claimed that Khodayarifard was a member of the [Basij](2892082.md). The governor of Kuhdasht, an [Imam of Friday Prayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imam_of_Friday_Prayer), and [Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps](1285472.md) (IRGC) members visited Khodayarifard's family. Permission for the family to access Khodayarifard's body and carry out a burial was conditioned on the family making a televised statement stating that Khodayarifard had been a member of the Basij.[^cite_note-Hengaw-2026b-153] Governmental authorities gave warnings on [Telegram](41501305.md) and [Instagram](31591547.md) forbidding the publication of information about Khodayarifard's killing.[^cite_note-Hengaw-2026b-153] As of 5 January 2026, the authorities, who were pressuring Khodayarifard's family to state that he was a Basij member, had not given the body to Khodayarifard's family, according to *[IranWire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IranWire)*.[^cite_note-Aida_Ghajar-2026-487]

#### 1 January

See also: [Killing of Shayan Asadollahi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Shayan_Asadollahi)

Two protesters, Ahmad Jalil, 21, and Sajjad Valamanesh, 28, were killed in [Lordegan](3739011.md) on 1 January 2026. Both had been shot by security forces and died later from their injuries.[^cite_note-489][^cite_note-490] On the evening of 1 January, two men and a teenage boy, Shayan Asadollahi, 30, Vahab Musavi, and Mostafa, 15, residents of [Azna](1968991.md) (in Lorestan province) were killed by gunfire from security forces.[^cite_note-Hengaw-2026a-168] The IRGC-aligned *[Fars News Agency](5000099.md)* stated that the protesters had either tried to attack a police station[^cite_note-491] or had tried to disarm the security forces.[^cite_note-Hengaw-2026a-168] Ahmadreza Amani, 28, was shot in the chest by security forces at around 18\:00 [IRST](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Standard_Time) in Azna and died in hospital.[^cite_note-492]

Khodadad Shirvani, 33, a [Marvdasht](3252239.md) resident, was shot with shotgun pellets by security forces on the same evening in Marvdasht. He died after being transferred to a hospital.[^cite_note-493] In [Nurabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nurabad,_Lorestan) (Lorestan province), Ahad Ebrahimpour Abdoli, 35, was lethally shot the same evening with three bullets (one to his heart) by security forces during a protest in Ba'ath Square in Nurabad. Security forces and the Imam of Friday Prayer pressured Abdoli's family to say that he was a Basij member and that he had been shot by "enemy forces".[^cite_note-494]

#### 2 January

On 2 January, a 42-year-old protester, Ali Azizi Jafarabadi, a Kurdish man from [Harsin County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harsin_County) was shot dead by security forces in [Harsin](9590172.md).[^cite_note-495]

#### 3 January

See also: [2026 Malekshahi massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Malekshahi_massacre), [Killing of Latif Karimi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Latif_Karimi), [Killing of Reza Ghanbari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Reza_Ghanbari), and [Killing of Saghar Etemadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Saghar_Etemadi)

On 3 January, the total number of arrested protesters had increased to 132 according to *[Hengaw](72014392.md)*[^cite_note-496] or 582 according to *[HRANA](39793882.md)*.[^cite_note-Human_Rights_Activists_in_Iran_News_Agency-2026a-172] *[Iran International](58555280.md)* estimated the death count of protesters to be at least eight, the number of locations to be 113 locations in 46 cities across 22 provinces, with at least 44 people shot and wounded by live ammunition or pellet guns fired by Iranian security forces.[^cite_note-Iran_International-2025e-132][^cite_note-497] [Four protesters were shot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Malekshahi_massacre) dead with "military-grade" weapons by IRGC members at protests in [Malekshahi County](24131946.md) in [Ilam province](448068.md); forty were injured and many taken to hospital.[^cite_note-498]

#### 4 January

By the early morning of 4 January, *Iran International* reported the death toll from the protests to have risen to at least 16.[^cite_note-499] *HRANA* estimated that since the beginning of the protests there had been 990 arrests and 51 cases of injuries to protesters, mostly from pellet and plastic bullets.[^cite_note-Human_Rights_Activists_in_Iran_News_Agency-2026b-179]

#### 5 January

The total number of arrested protesters rose to 1,200 on 5 January.[^cite_note-500] Iranian authorities claimed to have arrested a [Mossad](27619007.md) agent partaking in the protests, with the agent allegedly confessed to being recruited, trained by, and continuing communication with Mossad, and said that Mossad handlers told him to go to people's residences, but was later instructed to move his "operations" to local marketplaces.[^cite_note-501]

#### 6 January

On 6 January, a total of 2,076 protesters had been arrested, and at least 34 protesters and 2 police officers had been killed, according to *HRANA*.[^cite_note-Human_Rights_Activists_in_Iran_News_Agency-2026c-189]

#### 7 January

*HRANA* estimated 140 new arrests of protesters or identifications of previously arrested protesters, making a total of 2,217, including 165 minors and 46 university students. *HRANA* counted at total since the beginning of the protests as 38 deaths, including 29 adult protesters, 5 minor protesters, and 4 security officers.[^cite_note-Human_Rights_Activists_in_Iran_News_Agency-2026d-193]

#### 8 January

According to reports, a massacre occurred in [Fardis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fardis) on 8 January 2026, where troops reportedly killed 50 protesters with a machine gun.[^cite_note-GulfNews_45_killed-502]

#### 9 January

See also: [2026 Rasht massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Rasht_massacre)

On 9 January, *HRANA* estimated that a total of 2,311 protesters had been arrested and at least 65 were dead.[^cite_note-HRANA-2026-1] [Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\)) reported that they were in contact with a Tehran-based doctor who informed them that over 217 protester deaths had been recorded across six hospitals in the city, while Iranian Nobel Peace Prize laureate [Shirin Ebadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shirin_Ebadi) warned that authorities might carry out a massacre under the cover of the widespread [internet blackout](82063825.md).[^cite_note-Jamalpour-2026-220][^cite_note-Iran_International-2026e-221] Reports from two hospitals in [Rasht](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rasht) and Tehran indicate overall 110 dead bodies being transferred to these two hospitals during 8 and 9 January. Some wounded people had military-grade bullets in head and neck, indicating that the shootings were intended to kill.[^cite_note-503]

#### 10 January

Amidst the [internet blackout](82063825.md) during the protests, *[Iran International](58555280.md)* stated their most conservative estimates indicated that at least 2,000 people had been killed by government forces over the past 48 hours alone.[^cite_note-Iran_International-2026h-75] On 10 January, *HRANA* estimated that 2,638 protesters had been arrested and confirmed that 116 fatalities had occurred.[^cite_note-504] The [Centre for Human Rights in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Human_Rights_in_Iran) warned that a "massacre is unfolding." It said hundreds of protesters had been killed since the government cut off internet access, and security forces, [as in the past, shot people in the eyes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eye_injuries_during_2022_Iranian_protests) with metal pellets and rubber bullets. It reported that hospitals were overwhelmed, and that casualties continue to rise.[^cite_note-505]

Iranian state media reported that [Artesh](48259216.md) First Lieutenant Amin Salimi was shot and killed "while on mission" in [Khavaran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khavaran_District) neighbourhood, eastern Tehran. US-based human rights activists acknowledged his death on 20 January.[^cite_note-\:15-226]

#### 11 January

According to a US-based rights group, more than 500 people have been killed in Iran's protests, with 579 additional deaths under investigation (raising the total to 1,123), while over 10,681 people have been arrested.[^cite_note-506] The [People's Mojahedin Organisation of Iran](285632.md) reported that more than 3,000 people had been killed in the protests by 11 January. Their figures, based on local sources, hospitals, and families, show the government even displayed some bodies on state TV, falsely blaming protesters for their deaths.[^cite_note-507][^cite_note-508] By 11 January, *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\))* reported that, starting with reports from a handful of Tehran hospitals, an informal, expatriate group of academics and professionals calculated that protester deaths could have reached 6,000 through Saturday the 10th.[^cite_note-Vick-2026-509]

#### 12 January

On 12 January, *[CNN](62028.md)* reported that given the [government's internet shutdown](82063825.md) and the slow trickle of information emerging from Iran, the full scale of casualties remains unclear.[^cite_note-510]

#### 13 January

On 13 January, *[Iran International](58555280.md)* reported that at least 12,000 people had been killed, describing the massacre as the "largest [killing in Iranian contemporary history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_massacres_in_Iran)".[^cite_note-Iran_International-2026j-247] [CBS News](359551.md) reported that activist groups in Iran estimated 12,000 people to have been killed, and possibly 20,000, based on medical reports.[^cite_note-CBS_News-2026b-248]

#### 15 January

By 15 January, fatality reports saw a significant increase as minimal internet connectivity was restored.[^cite_note-\:3-511] While confirmed figures from *HRANA* stood at approximately 2,000-2,500 deaths,[^cite_note-512][^cite_note-\:3-511] other rights organisations such as [Iran Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Human_Rights) (IHR) reported at least 3,428 fatalities.[^cite_note-\:7-442] At the same time, leaked internal documents from the [Supreme National Security Council](6295948.md) and the presidential office, reported by [Iran International](58555280.md), suggested that as many as 12,000 people may have been killed during the peak crackdown between 8 and 10 January.[^cite_note-513] [CBS News](359551.md) cited sources within Iran claiming the total death toll could potentially reach 20,000.[^cite_note-514][^cite_note-515]

#### 17 January

On 17 January, *[The Sunday Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Sunday_Times)* reported that "at least 16,500-18,000" people had been killed, and 330,000 had been injured.[^cite_note-SundayTimes17Jan-55][^cite_note-330000injured-516]

#### 18 January

On 18 January, [HRANA](39793882.md) stated that 24,669 protesters had been detained, at least 25 children under 18 years of age had been killed during the protests, and 145 [forced confessions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forced_confessions) had been broadcast.[^cite_note-hrana-jan18-447] The U.S.-based Human Rights Activists News Agency (*HRANA)* added that 3,919 people were verified to have been killed during the protests, and a further 8,949 additional deaths were still under investigation.[^cite_note-hrana-jan18-447] *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* reported claims that detainees were being subjected to torture and sexual assault while in custody;[^cite_note-sexual-assault-480] *[Iran International](58555280.md)* reported detainees were being injected with unknown chemical substances.[^cite_note-chemical-injections-466]

#### 20 January

On 20 January, the Canadian-based [International Centre for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Centre_for_Human_Rights) reported that at least 43,000 had been killed by Islamic Republic agents, over 350,000 injured, and over 10,000 blinded in a [similar tactic from the 2022 protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eye_injuries_during_2022_Iranian_protests).[^cite_note-ichr-20jan-56] According to the report, more than 95% of the victims were killed on 8 and 9 January amid the internet blackout.[^cite_note-ichr-20jan-56]

#### 22 January

On 22 January, [Iranian state media](819903.md) released its first official casualty figures related to the protests, reporting that 3,117 people had been killed. The announcement stated that the dead included both civilians and members of the security forces, but provided no detailed breakdown or methodology for how the figures were compiled.[^cite_note-517][^cite_note-518]

Independent human rights organisations challenged the official casualty figures. [HRANA](39793882.md) reported that the death toll had surpassed 4,900, based on information compiled from hospital records, eyewitness testimony, and regional monitoring networks, and warned that the true number of fatalities was likely higher due to severe reporting constraints.[^cite_note-519] HRANA later updated its data to state that the verified total had reached at least 4,519 confirmed deaths with an additional 9,049 cases under review.

On the same day, the [United Nations Special Rapporteur on Human Rights in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Special_Rapporteur_on_Human_Rights_in_Iran) stated the number of civilians killed may exceeded 20,000, citing information received from civil society sources and the cumulative scale of the crackdown.[^cite_note-un-sr-520] Separately, the U.S. television network [NewsNation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NewsNation) reported substantially higher estimates, suggesting the death toll could be closer to 30,000.[^cite_note-newsnation-jan-22-521]

HRANA, alongside other journalists and independent analysts emphasised that discrepancies between official and independent casualty figures were driven in part by near-total internet shutdowns, restricted information flows, limits on foreign media access, and security pressure on local reporters. These conditions, including arrests and intimidation of journalists, significantly hindered the ability of independent monitors to document fatalities comprehensively and to verify casualty data during the protests.[^cite_note-522][^cite_note-523]

#### 24 January

The *HRANA* total death count estimate, including both verified cases and deaths under investigation, grew above 20,000 on 24 January, with counts of 5,149 adult protesters, 60 minors, 208 security force members, 42 non-participants, and 17,031 deaths under investigation, making 22,490 in total. *HRANA* stated that the overall level of repression was unprecedented, with a cumulative number of 205 broadcast false confessions and the total number of arrests reaching 40,887.[^cite_note-HRANA_28thday-524]

#### 25 January

On 25 January, *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\))* reported the 8 and 9 January protest-related death toll as 30,304 from hospital records, according to Amir Parasta, a doctor, excluding military hospital records, bodies in morgues, and places not included in the analysis. *Time* also stated that two senior health officials said that the death toll "could have been" 30,000, and that *Time* could not independently verify the two health officials' estimate.[^cite_note-Time_30k_local_health_officials-52][^cite_note-525]

The same day, *[Iran International](58555280.md)* reported more than 36,500 were killed by security forces on 8 and 9 January, based on documents reviewed by *Iran International'*&#x73; editorial board, as well as field reports and accounts from medical staff and victims' families.[^cite_note-36,500iranint-80]

#### 27 January

*[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* reported that the Islamic Republic was concealing protest deaths through [mass graves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_graves_in_Iran) and burials.[^cite_note-guardian-mass-burials-51][^cite_note-iran-intl-mass-burials-469][^cite_note-iran-intl-burials-445]

#### February

Arrests in relation to the protests continued in early February. Arrests of three politicians, [Azar Mansouri](54632737.md), [Ebrahim Asgharzadeh](7756323.md), and former Deputy Foreign Minister [Mohsen Aminzadeh](23312237.md), were announced on 8 February.[^cite_note-IranIntl_IRI_arrests_3_reformists-526] Reports also emerged in February about the detention of Shin-Nosuke Kawashima, the head of the [NHK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NHK) office in Tehran, and his transfer to [Evin Prison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evin_Prison).[^cite_note-527] The government of [Japan](15573.md) confirmed the detention of one of its citizens in Tehran and stated that Tokyo authorities were in contact with the detainee and his family, demanding his swift release. Iranian officials have not yet officially confirmed the arrest. NHK stated that the safety of its staff is a priority, and no further information has been provided on the matter.[^cite_note-528]

#### March

Amid the [Iran war](82533582.md) and the ongoing [internet blackout](82063825.md), 466 people were [arrested](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrests_during_the_2025-2026_Iran_protests_and_2026_Iran_war?action=edit\&redlink=1) in Iran on 24 March for their online activities, with over 1,000 arrests having been made over the course of the month since the start of the war, pertaining to individuals accused of "filming sensitive locations", "sharing anti-government content online", or "cooperating with the enemy".[^cite_note-529][^cite_note-530]

#### Executions

See also: [2026 Iran massacres § Executions](82036668.md#Executions)

According to an [HRANA](39793882.md) report on 16 January, Iran carried out at least 52 executions during the protests between 5 and 14 January.[^cite_note-52-executions-531][^cite_note-hrana-52-532] On 21 January, HRANA further added that at least 313 prisoners were executed during the time of the protests.[^cite_note-313-executions-533]

On 12 January 2026, it was reported that 26-year-old protester, [Erfan Soltani](82114564.md), was sentenced to be executed on 14 January, making him one of the first of these protesters to be handed an [execution sentence](5170842.md).[^cite_note-Isaacson-2026-534] Soltani was arrested during protests in [Fardis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fardis) on 8 January.[^cite_note-Isaacson-2026-534][^cite_note-535] Human rights groups and activists reported that Soltani was denied access to a lawyer, a [fair trial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_to_a_fair_trial), or any opportunity to appeal.[^cite_note-Isaacson-2026-534] Several days later, Soltani was notified that he was to face execution on 14 January, less than a week after his arrest.[^cite_note-\:13-536] Shortly before the execution date, Iranian Foreign Minister Abbas Araghchi denied any plans to hang people, and Soltani's family was notified that his execution had been postponed, while the judiciary stated that the charges against him only consisted of "colluding against national security" and "propaganda activities against the establishment" which are not punishable by death in Iran.[^cite_note-\:13-536] The state broadcasting company IRIB said that reports of Soltani's pending execution were a "blatant act of news fabrication."[^cite_note-\:13-536] According to Human Rights Organisation Hangaw, Soltani met with his family on 16 January and was reported to be in good health. By 2 February, it was reported that Soltani had been released on bail.[^cite_note-537]

On 16 January, U.S. president Donald Trump thanked the government for promising to stop 800 planned executions;[^cite_note-trump-thank-1-538][^cite_note-trump-thank-2-539] however, Ali Salehi, the State Prosecutor in Tehran was seen on state television saying: "Trump says a lot of nonsense and gibberish", and "Our reaction will be forceful, preventive and swift. Indictments have been issued for numerous cases and sent to courts."[^cite_note-540] Iran's judiciary chief, Gholamhossein Mohseni Ejei, stated that carrying out the sentences quickly would serve as a deterrent.[^cite_note-541] On 19 January 2026, UN rights chief [Volker Türk](71721726.md) claimed in a statement that Iran was using executions "as a tool of state intimidation". During 2025, 1,500 people [had been executed in Iran](5170842.md). The number of executions in 2025 was second only to [China](1461479.md).[^cite_note-542]

On 17 January, another protester, a 19-year-old Amirhossein Ghaderzadeh, was sentenced to death by hanging, with his execution scheduled for 21 January.[^cite_note-ghaderzadeh-543]

On 20 January, reports circulated that the Iran Human Rights Society (IHRS) said that an Iranian conscript named [Javid Khales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javid_Khales?action=edit\&redlink=1) had been sentenced to death after refusing an order to fire on protesters and that he was being held in [Isfahan Prison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isfahan_Central_Prison), though this has not been independently verified.[^cite_note-544][^cite_note-545]

On 22 January, 33-year-old Ali Rahbar was reportedly executed in [Mashhad](406512.md), becoming one of the first protesters to be executed.[^cite_note-546][^cite_note-547] Iran's judiciary denied his execution having taken place, stating that such a person had not even been detained.[^cite_note-548]

According to activists, human rights advocates, and foreign intelligence, the Islamic Republic secretly executed jailed protesters while disguising the deaths as protest casualties and suicides.[^cite_note-secret-executions-1-549][^cite_note-secret-executions-2-550][^cite_note-secret-executions-3-551][^cite_note-secret-executions-4-552][^cite_note-553][^cite_note-sea-of-blood-554][^cite_note-555] There were also reports of detainees being injected with unknown substances, leading to their deaths.[^cite_note-injection-death-468][^cite_note-sea-of-blood-554]

On 6 February, *[The Independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Independent)* reported that secret executions of detained protesters had taken place in [Tehran](57654.md), [Isfahan](223149.md), [Shiraz](39622.md), and [Mashhad](406512.md), and that mass executions were being planned by the Islamic Republic once [negotiations with the United States](82251337.md#Negotiations) ended.[^cite_note-mass-executions-556] On 9 February, it was reported that the Islamic Republic was pressuring the families of detainees to attend pro-government rallies planned for 22 Bahman (11 February), marking the [anniversary of the 1979 Islamic Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anniversary_of_the_Iranian_Revolution), telling them that only if they complied and attended would their detained relatives be spared execution or have their sentences reduced.[^cite_note-pro-gov-rally-coercion-451]

On 16 February, it was reported that the Islamic Republic had issued death sentences for at least sixteen detained protesters, with multiple detainees stating they had been tortured and were compelled to sign [forced confessions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forced_confession).[^cite_note-16-feb-death-sentences-557] Norway-based rights group [Hengaw](72014392.md) stated the cases are marred by [due process](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Due_process) violations.[^cite_note-16-feb-death-sentences-557] [HRANA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HRANA) stated on 15 February, that 53,552 had been arrested, while [Iran International](58555280.md) added that many are facing serious charges.[^cite_note-16-feb-death-sentences-557][^cite_note-hrana-15-feb-53]

Reports also indicated detained protesters were being brutally tortured and raped before being killed in prison, including women's bodies being returned to their families with missing wombs in order to hide the crimes.[^cite_note-wombs-482]

On 19 March, three more protesters were executed, including teenage wrestler [Saleh Mohammadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saleh_Mohammadi).[^cite_note-558][^cite_note-saleh-mohammadi-337][^cite_note-559] On 2 April, another protester, 18-year-old Amir Hossein Hatami, was executed.[^cite_note-560] On 5 April, two more protesters were executed.[^cite_note-561][^cite_note-562]

On 13 May, one man was executed after being accused of killing a police officer during the protests. According to HRANA, the regime didn't allow him access to an independent lawyer.[^cite_note-563]

According to Amnesty International, as of 26 May, "At least 78 protesters, dissidents and others with real or perceived links to banned opposition groups are under sentence of death and at risk of execution."[^cite_note-564] In August 2026, [Euronews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euronews) reported that Iran executed a foreign national named Qaim Hosseini; he was accused of taking part in anti-government protests on 8 January in [Isfahan](223149.md).[^cite_note-565]

### Government forces

Government authorities repeatedly presented fatalities during the protests as members of the security forces killed by protesters, after which evidence from witnesses and family statements showed that the victim had been shot by the security forces.[^cite_note-Hengaw-2025-566] Government media claimed that Amirhesam Khodayarifard, killed on 31 December, was a member of the [Basij](2892082.md).[^cite_note-Associated_Press-2026-155] Eyewitness reports and video evidence, collected by *[Hengaw](72014392.md)*, contradicted this claim, in particular showing that he was standing among the protesters and was killed by a shot to the head by a security forces member.[^cite_note-Hengaw-2026b-153] Initially, authorities conditioned family access to Khodayarifard's body on the family making a televised statement that he was a Basij member.[^cite_note-Hengaw-2026b-153] During the funeral, which took place on 2 January, Khodayarifard's father confirmed that his son was not a Basij member.[^cite_note-Iran_International-2026k-170]

On 3 January, *[Agence France-Presse](471981.md)* referred to a statement by *[Mehr](2707394.md)* that IRGC member Latif Karimi was killed during clashes in [Malekshahi County](24131946.md),[^cite_note-567] during which four protesters were killed by the IRGC.[^cite_note-568] On 4 January, eyewitnesses and other sources clarified that Karimi was present among the protesters when he was shot by IRGC members, and died in Imam Khomeini Hospital in [Ilam](482372.md). Karimi was a retired [brigadier-general](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brigadier-general) by profession. Karimi's son stated on [Telegram](41501305.md), "My father's only 'crime' was telling \[the government security forces] not to shoot at the people."[^cite_note-Hengaw-2025-566]

On 7 January, militants of the [Baloch nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baloch_nationalist) militant organisation [People's Fighters Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaysh_al-Adl#People's_Fighters_Front) (PFF) assassinated Mahmoud Haqiqat, the police chief of [Iranshahr](1853198.md).[^cite_note-caliber.az-2026-23][^cite_note-Deutsche_Welle-24][^cite_note-Noonan-2026-186] IRGC-affiliated media reported that protesters killed two [Law Enforcement Command](5187924.md) officers during protests in Lordegan as well as an unspecified security force member in Malekshahi.[^cite_note-Noonan-2026-186]

On 8 January, Norway-based human rights organisation [Hengaw](72014392.md) claimed that two [IRGC Ground Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IRGC_Ground_Forces) members were killed during the protests in [Kermanshah](1489244.md).[^cite_note-Glynn-2026-119] A police officer in [Malard County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malard_County) at the Tehran province was killed from a stabbing after attempts to control local unrest.[^cite_note-Agence_France_Presse-2026-203]

On 9 January, opposition media reported that clashes between protesters and security forces in Kermanshah Province killed at least 10 IRGC Ground Forces [Nabi Akram Unit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kermanshah_Nabi_Akram_Corps) members.[^cite_note-Bern-2026a-215]

On 11 January, fighters from the PFF killed one Law Enforcement Command officer and injured another in an attack on an LEC patrol vehicle in [Dashtiari County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dashtiari_County), Sistan and Baluchistan Province.[^cite_note-IranUpdateJanuary11-27]

On 17 March, [Iran International](58555280.md) reported that security forces were beginning to grow frustrated with the system with some signs of defections.[^cite_note-\:25-569]
