### Foreign victims

Canadian foreign minister [Anita Anand](61752870.md) confirmed that a Canadian citizen was killed by the Islamic Republic forces during the protests.[^cite_note-574] Several [Afghan migrants](3657017.md) were also among those killed in the protests.[^cite_note-575]

## Reactions

Main article: [Reactions to the 2025–2026 Iranian protests](82038636.md)



[Iranian diaspora](9573713.md) holding a solidarity rally in [Perth, Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perth,_Australia), on 10 January 2026 against the Islamic Republic

Reactions to the protests ranged from calls for dialogue and economic relief to warnings of force:

### Domestic

Supreme Leader Ali Khamenei said officials should talk to protesters, but added that "rioters must be put in their place".[^cite_note-576] As the unrest continued, President Masoud Pezeshkian announced economic measures including changes to foreign-exchange subsidies intended to shift support directly to consumers.[^cite_note-577] Pezeshkian later apologized to the nation for the massacres.[^cite_note-auto9-83] Vice President [Mohammad Reza Aref](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Reza_Aref) attempted to resign during the first days of the protests, but it was not accepted by President Pezeshkian.[^cite_note-578] [Mohammad Baqer Qalibaf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Baqer_Qalibaf), Speaker of the [Islamic Consultative Assembly](383276.md), said "Malicious individuals and organised movements want to turn any kind of public demand and protest into chaos and chaos using their trained agents in the square, but the Iranian nation has repeatedly demonstrated its vigilance, awareness, and compassion for the country's security".[^cite_note-579]

On 10 January 2026, the IRGC warned that safeguarding security is a "red line".[^cite_note-580] Chief Justice [Gholam-Hossein Mohseni-Eje'i](3002270.md), the head of Iran's judiciary, said that the punishment for rioters would "be decisive, maximum and without any legal leniency".[^cite_note-581] Attorney general [Mohammad Movahedi Azad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Movahedi_Azad) warned that anyone taking part in demonstrations is an "[enemy of God](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hirabah#Iran)", a crime that carries the death penalty under Iranian law.[^cite_note-582]

On 19 January, [Ahmad-Reza Radan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmad-Reza_Radan), Iran's national police chief stated that those who were "deceived" into taking part in the demonstrations, or "riots" as the government defines them, have three days to turn themselves in.[^cite_note-583] He also said: Important arrests were made of the main elements of the unrest, who will be punished after going through legal procedures.[^cite_note-584] On 20 January the [Iranian Parliament](383276.md) threatened that an attack on [Supreme Leader](36814344.md), Ayatollah Ali Khamenei will lead to [Jihad](16203.md).[^cite_note-585] On 25 January, [Yousef Pezeshkian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yousef_Pezeshkian), one of president [Masoud Pezeshkian](2650383.md)'s sons, urged authorities to lift internet restrictions, arguing that blocking access would only delay, not prevent, the spread of images and videos showing the government's violent suppression of protesters.[^cite_note-586]

The Workers' Union of the Tehran and Suburbs Bus Company[^cite_note-587] said "the path to the liberation of workers and toilers does not lie in a leader imposed on the people from above, nor in reliance on foreign powers, nor through factions within the government."[^cite_note-588]

On 23 March, the Iranian judiciary said that there would be "no leniencies" for those with convicted with relation to the protests.[^cite_note-589]

### International

#### Iranian diaspora

Main article: [2026 Iranian diaspora protests](82164645.md)



Protest in [Gothenburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gothenburg), Sweden on 25 January 2026

The [Iranian diaspora](9573713.md) held solidarity rallies worldwide, with some facing [violence and threats during the demonstrations](82164645.md#Violence_towards_diaspora_protesters).[^cite_note-590][^cite_note-nbcla-240][^cite_note-591]

#### Intergovernmental organisations

-  [European Union](9317.md): The [European External Action Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_External_Action_Service) urged Iran's security forces to exercise restraint and called on authorities to uphold rights including freedom of expression and peaceful assembly.

  [^cite_note-592]

  &#x20;The EU also co-signed the aforementioned joint statement issued by Australia and Canada.

  [^cite_note-The_New_Region-593]

  - European Commission President [Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md), urged the release of imprisoned Iranian protesters, condemned the violent crackdown, and called for internet access to be restored, saying Europe stands "fully behind" those demonstrating.

    [^cite_note-594]

  - On 16 March 2026, the council of the European Union imposed new sanctions following human rights abuses during the January protests. Those sanctioned include senior officials such as Iran's Deputy Interior Minister for Security, commanders of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps (IRGC), judiciary members, and prison authorities, many of whom are accused of abuses including torture, forced confessions, unfair trials, and harsh sentencing of protesters, activists, journalists, and minorities. Also sanctioned were key organizations involved in repression, including IRGC-linked groups and Naji Research and Development Company (NRDC), which developed surveillance technology used to monitor citizens, as well as cyber police authorities responsible for censorship and online prosecutions. The sanctions include asset freezes, travel bans to the EU, and prohibitions on receiving funds from EU citizens or companies.

    [^cite_note-595]

- The  [United Nations](31769.md) criticised Iran's internet shutdown and violation of civil liberties. On 20 January it was stated that The [UN Human Rights Council](635790.md) would hold an emergency session on Iran on 23 January about the extreme violence in Iran.

  [^cite_note-596]

  [^cite_note-597]

  - UN Special Rapporteur [Mai Sato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mai_Sato) called for an independent investigation to determine whether the Iranian government's suppression of nationwide protests constitutes [crimes against humanity](61099.md).

    [^cite_note-598]

  - On 23 January 2026, the UN Human Rights Council (UNHRC) adopted a resolution condemning the violent suppression of protests in Iran. Twenty-five states voted in favour of the resolution, including [Spain](26667.md), [Italy](14532.md), [Slovenia](27338.md), [Mexico](3966054.md), [Colombia](5222.md), [South Korea](27019.md), and [Ghana](12067.md). Seven states voted against, including [China](5405.md), [India](14533.md), [Vietnam](202354.md), [Indonesia](14579.md), and [Pakistan](23235.md). Fourteen states abstained, including [Brazil](3383.md), [Qatar](8238258.md), [Egypt](8087628.md), [South Africa](17416221.md), [Kenya](188171.md), and [Thailand](30128.md).

    [^cite_note-599]

    [^cite_note-600]

#### States

-  [Australia](4689264.md) and  [Canada](5042916.md) issued a joint statement condemning Iran's use of force against protesters.

  [^cite_note-The_New_Region-593]

-  [China](5405.md) – On 23 January 2026, China voted against a [UN Human Rights Council](635790.md) resolution that condemned Iran's crackdown on nationwide protests.

  [^cite_note-aljazeera-2026-1-24-601]

-  [France](5843419.md),  [Germany](11867.md) and the  [United Kingdom](31717.md)'s [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md), [Friedrich Merz](1967626.md) and [Keir Starmer](18681491.md), respectively, released a joint statement on 9 January urging Iran to exercise restraint.

  [^cite_note-Christou-2026b-78]

  - Chancellor Friedrich Merz separately spoke about the violence against Iranian protesters: "This violence is not an expression of strength, but rather a sign of weakness. This violence must end",

    [^cite_note-602]

    &#x20;later saying "If a regime can only keep itself in power by force, then it's effectively at the end. I believe we are now seeing the final days and weeks of this regime. In any case, it has no legitimacy through elections in the population. The population is now rising up against this regime."

    [^cite_note-603]

    [^cite_note-604]

-  [Holy See](13393.md) – [Pope Leo XIV](54520372.md) expressed concern.

  [^cite_note-605]

-  [India](14533.md) – On 23 January 2026, India voted against a UNHRC resolution condemning the crackdown on nationwide protests in Iran.

  [^cite_note-aljazeera-2026-1-24-601]

-  [Israel](9282173.md) – Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) said Israel "\[identifies] with the struggle of the Iranian people for freedom, liberty and justice". He also mentioned the possibility that the people would take their fate into their hands.

  [^cite_note-606]

  - [Mossad](27619007.md), Israel's [intelligence agency](81014.md), said it was "with \[the Iranian protesters] in the field".

    [^cite_note-Mossad-607]

-  [New Zealand](4913064.md) – Foreign Minister [Winston Peters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winston_Peters) expressed concern about the killing of protesters and described protests as a "fundamental human right".

  [^cite_note-608]

-  [Poland](22936.md) – On 15 January 2026, the [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](1161355.md) summoned the Iranian ambassador to express concern over the growing number of casualties and arrests, and called on Iran to cease violence against the protesters and initiate talks.

  [^cite_note-609]

-  [Spain](26667.md) – The [government of Pedro Sánchez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_government_of_Pedro_S%C3%A1nchez) condemned the violence against demonstrators in Iran.

  [^cite_note-610]

-  [Ukraine](31750.md) – On 2 February 2026, Ukraine designated the IRGC as a terrorist organisation, citing the violent crackdown on protestors.

  [^cite_note-611]

-  [United Kingdom](31717.md) – On 13 January 2026, Foreign Secretary Yvette Cooper summoned the Iranian ambassador over the mass killings.

  [^cite_note-612]

  &#x20;British Secretary of State for Transport [Heidi Alexander](27306797.md) told [Sky News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_News) that the current priority is to "stem the violence" in Iran. She said Iran is a threat to the Middle East and represses its own people.

  [^cite_note-Mason-2026-613]

  - [Kemi Badenoch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kemi_Badenoch), leader of the opposition [Conservative Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Party_\(UK\)), told the [BBC](19344654.md) that she would "not have an issue" with Iranian regime change and said she supported the involvement of the US and its allies. She claimed that Iran posed a direct threat to the UK, saying it would "very happily wipe out the UK if it felt it could get away with it".

    [^cite_note-Mason-2026-613]

-  [United States](3434750.md) – US president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) warned that the United States would intervene if Iranian authorities violently suppressed "peaceful protests",

  [^cite_note-614]

  &#x20;and stated that the US was "locked and loaded and ready to go".

  [^cite_note-615]

  &#x20;Trump later said that US is considering "very strong options" as a response to the Iran protests, among them possible military intervention, and he said: "we will hit them at levels that they've never been hit before".

  [^cite_note-616]

  &#x20;On 15 January, multiple sources reported that [Carrier Strike Group 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carrier_Strike_Group_3) was expected to arrive in the Middle East in "about a week", and *The New York Times* reported that an array of combat and refuelling aircraft "were expected to start flowing into the region soon".

  [^cite_note-617]

  [^cite_note-618]

  &#x20;On 16 January, Trump thanked the Iranian government for promising to stop the 800 planned executions.

  [^cite_note-trump-thank-1-538]

  [^cite_note-trump-thank-2-539]

  &#x20;[Steve Witkoff](40901053.md), the U.S. Special Envoy to the Middle East, reportedly held a secret meeting with [Reza Pahlavi](613947.md), the exiled former Crown Prince of Iran.

  [^cite_note-619]

  &#x20;On 26 January, U.S. officials said the aircraft carrier [USS *Abraham Lincoln*](199106.md) and its escorting warships [entered the Middle East region](82251337.md).

  [^cite_note-620]

  [^cite_note-621]

  &#x20;Defence Ministry spokesman [Reza Talaei-Nik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reza_Talaei-Nik), said Iran would respond "forcefully" to any US or Israeli attack.

  [^cite_note-622]

  &#x20;On 13 February 2026, United States Treasury Secretary [Scott Bessent](38532044.md) claimed that Washington engineered a dollar shortage in Iran to send the Iranian rial into freefall and cause protests in Iran.

  [^cite_note-623]

-  [Turkey](11125639.md) – Foreign minister [Hakan Fidan](39996069.md) stated that the protests in Iran were "being manipulated from abroad by Iran's rivals", including the United States and Israel, expressed a wish for the resolution of the perceived antagonism between Iran and the West through negotiations, and called on Iran to engage in "very genuine reconciliation and cooperation" with other Middle Eastern countries.

  [^cite_note-624]

#### NGOs

- [Amnesty International](18947898.md) and [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) condemned the violent repression and documented indiscriminate killings and arrests.

  [^cite_note-625]

  [^cite_note-626]

  &#x20;Amnesty International later stated in a report marking four years since the [Mahsa Amini protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahsa_Amini_protests) that the Iranian regime coordinated the killing, torture, and sexual abuse of protesters and bystanders during the crackdown on [Mahsa Amini protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahsa_Amini_protests), and Tehran's [suppression](82036668.md) of the 2025–2026 Iranian protests bore the "same hallmarks" but on a larger scale.

  [^cite_note-627]

  [^cite_note-628]

#### Others

- The Mobarizoun Popular Front, a newly-formed [Baloch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baloch_people) nationalist organisation, expressed its support for the protests.

  [^cite_note-Institute_for_the_Study_of_War-2025-25]

- The [Army of the Men of the Naqshbandi Order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Army_of_the_Men_of_the_Naqshbandi_Order) expressed support for the Iranian opposition against the Iranian government in early January, calling the Iranian government a "fraudulent [mullah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mullah) regime".

  [^cite_note-629]

- The [Kurdistan Free Life Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdistan_Free_Life_Party) (PJAK) co-chair Amir Kerimi stated that the Islamic Republic had lost its social [legitimacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_legitimacy) and described the situation as an opportunity to build "a democratic and [decentralised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decentralised) Iran ... that transcends the [nation-state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nation-state), based on the [self-governance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-governance) of the people", while indicating "the construction of a new authoritarian regime through Reza Pahlavi" as his biggest concern.

  [^cite_note-630]

  - Israel's former defence minister, [Yoav Gallant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Gallant), stated "At this time, when what matters is the action of the masses on the ground, we need to stay behind and direct things with an invisible hand".

    [^cite_note-631]

#### Corporate organisations

From 9 January 2026, multiple international airlines suspended or cancelled flights to and from Iran due to the protests, the internet blackout, and security concerns. [Turkish Airlines](167925.md) cancelled flights between [Istanbul](3391396.md) and Iranian cities including Tehran, Tabriz and Mashhad, while [AJet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AJet) and [Pegasus Airlines](1597891.md) suspended all services to Iran. UAE carriers [flydubai](18432521.md) and [Emirates](142715.md) halted flights to Iranian destinations, and [Qatar Airways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qatar_Airways) cancelled several flights from Doha to Iran. In Europe, [Austrian Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austrian_Airlines) cancelled its Vienna-Tehran flight on 9 January. [Lufthansa](97733.md), which had planned to resume flights on 16 January after it suspended them due to regional security concerns, delayed its plans to resume services due to the protests.[^cite_note-632][^cite_note-633][^cite_note-634][^cite_note-Farhat-2026-219][^cite_note-635]

#### International travel advisories

Since the outbreak of the protests, several countries have issued travel advisories or warnings for Iran and have advised their citizens to leave the country. These advisories cite security concerns and potential disruptions to transportation and communications. Countries that have issued such advisories include the United States,[^cite_note-636] the United Kingdom,[^cite_note-\:22-264] Canada,[^cite_note-637] Australia,[^cite_note-638] Germany,[^cite_note-639] France,[^cite_note-640] New Zealand,[^cite_note-641] Ireland,[^cite_note-642] and India.[^cite_note-643]

#### Polling

A January 2026 Quinnipiac poll of US voters found that 70% oppose US military involvement in Iran, 79% of Democrats, 80% of independents and 53% of Republicans opposed military involvement.[^cite_note-644][^cite_note-645][^cite_note-646]

## Analysis

On 30 December, *Iran International* suggested the protests were a "historic break" of Iranian bazaar merchants, usually a key ally of the Islamic Republic, from the government. This break was fuelled by the Islamic Republic's blame towards the merchants as "price gougers" for rejecting state-standardised pricing, and being unable to restock market inventories if they complied to their demands. Anger towards the government by merchants was also caused by the proposed [2025–26 Iranian budget](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025%E2%80%9326_Iranian_budget), which would prioritise [deficit spending](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deficit_spending) and tax increases to make up for a decline in oil revenues.[^cite_note-647] *Iran International* cited the opinion of [intelligence analysts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intelligence_analysis) and journalists, who suggested Iran might have entered the early stages of regime collapse.[^cite_note-648] On 4 January, Iranian officials, including foreign minister [Abbas Araghchi](21951274.md), described the government as being in a "survival mode", with difficulties in either reversing economic problems or handling the threat of attack by the US or Israel. Pezeshkian held emergency meetings following the start of the protests. Some advisers recommended he publicly criticise the role of Khamenei as supreme leader.[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-396] A 5 January analysis argued the 2025–26 protests differ from the [Mahsa Amini protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahsa_Amini_protests), in that the former include small towns rather than just cities, and involve a broader range of groups, including students, workers, women, and ethnic minorities. The analysis saw differences in the international context as significant, with Trump's unpredictability and willingness to violate international law as differing from Biden, and the [fall of the Assad regime](78548066.md) as a weakening of Iran's regional support. The authors saw the focus of the 2025–2026 protests as shifting from social reform to regime change.[^cite_note-Golkar-2026-11] An analysis in [Iran International](58555280.md) argued that the fact that students conducted a walkout from high schools throughout the country in mid-February shows that members of Gen Z have not given up despite the attempts to suppress the protests with extensive force.[^cite_note-649]

In early January 2026, *[The Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times)* referred to intelligence reports stating that Khamenei had an escape plan, for him, 20 close associates and family members, including son [Mojtaba Khamenei](23324096.md), ready to flee to Moscow in case security forces defected to the protesters. [Beni Sabti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beni_Sabti?action=edit\&redlink=1), a former Israeli intelligence officer, stated he expected Moscow to be Khamenei's preferred location of exile.[^cite_note-Weiniger-2026-409] Social scientist Mali Rezaei viewed the protests as showing a "deepening rupture between society and the ideological foundations of the state". She argued that one of the factors was the context of Iran's [multi-millennial history](14653.md#Antiquity) and [ethnic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnicities_in_Iran) and [cultural](965233.md) diversity, in which the seventh century CE [Muslim conquest of Persia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_conquest_of_Persia) left in place "a persistent resistance to absolutism". She pointed to a 2020 GAMAAN study that found that [irreligion in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion_in_Iran) and support for [secularism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secularism_in_Iran) was growing. Rezaei saw environmental crises such as the [disappearance of Lake Urmia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Urmia#Falling_level_and_increasing_salinity) as playing a role. She viewed the government's destruction of elements of Persian culture and a "passivity in defending \[cultural] legacy" as a weakening of the government's protection of "the nation". Rezaei saw the Mahsa Amini protests and the [Woman, Life, Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woman,_Life,_Freedom) slogan as a turning point in which the protest movement evolved to avoid cooptation by either the government or celebrities. She viewed the pro-Pahlavi slogans as mainly representing a desire for a secular democracy, not absolute monarchy, that would recover national [agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agency_\(sociology\)).[^cite_note-650]

On 26 January, *[The New York Times](30680.md)* cited US intelligence reports viewing Iran's government as being in its weakest position since the 1979 [Iranian Revolution](347268.md). The *NYT* stated that a US military buildup near Iran was ongoing and viewed it as unclear if the US administration had taken a decision for a military attack.[^cite_note-651]

### View as an uprising

On 10 January, human rights activist Hamid Enayat suggested that "dozens of instances" of disarmament of security forces during the protests, and the "breakdown of the deterrent function" of the state's [monopoly of violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monopoly_of_violence) indicated a [transition to a new phase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Complex_system_approach_to_peace_and_armed_conflict#Phase_transitions), that of an [uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uprising). Enayat cited cases of protesters stopping security forces on buses, disarming them and tying their hands, and a case of a stun gun being taken from a security forces member and used against him. He argued that [Malekshahi County](24131946.md) effectively came under insurgent control on 6 January when security forces fled. He saw the protests as having shifted to a phase in which the state had lost its power to frighten citizens into obedience.[^cite_note-Hamid_Enayat-2026-652]

On 11 January, historian [Mark Almond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Almond) disagreed with comparison of the Iranian protests to the [fall of the Berlin Wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_the_Berlin_Wall) in 1989. He argued it would be more like the [Storming of the Bastille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Storming_of_the_Bastille), because, if successful, the overthrow of the Islamic Republic would be a rebellion against an internally developed regime, rather than one imposed internationally. He argued the protests already qualified for the term "revolution".[^cite_note-653] In contradistinction most Israeli analysts believed the conditions for successful revolution had not yet been met, the existential threat to the regime notwithstanding.[^cite_note-654][^cite_note-655] A minority view, such as held by retired [Brigadier General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brigadier_General) [Amir Avivi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amir_Avivi), chairman of the Israel Defence and Security Forum (IDSF), is that the Iranian regime faces imminent collapse.[^cite_note-656]

### View as the beginning of regime change

On 13 January, German Chancellor [Friedrich Merz](1967626.md) stated that "we are now witnessing the final days and weeks of this regime", arguing that "If a regime can only stay in power through violence, then it is effectively finished."[^cite_note-\:82-657] Historian Ali Ansari argued that while the Iranian regime has long relied on violence, it now faces a fragile state with dwindling resources and growing disenchantment. Ansari notes, "The Islamic Republic has few options that don't include its own demise", pointing to the rising anger and fear among the population, with many believing the regime has no future. This sentiment marks a turning point, as even the previously apathetic are now rejecting the system.[^cite_note-658] According to *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)*, Iran's leadership, having resorted to violence to maintain power, may be at breaking point. "Iran's rulers are merciless because of their weakness. They have nowhere to turn and nothing to offer their people but violence." It concluded this could be "the moment that ends the theocrats' 47 years in power".[^cite_note-659]

An analysis in *[Prospect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prospect_\(magazine\))* examined four scenarios of change if the government fell.[^cite_note-\:17-660][^cite_note-330000injured-516] [*HRANA*](39793882.md)'s total of confirmed deaths and deaths under investigation reached 22,490 on 24 January.[^cite_note-HRANA_28thday-524] According to one scenario, the removal of Khamenei from power would create a power vacuum, while senior bureaucrats would continue to run the state. Competition between remaining elites would bring about a temporary government, as a result of compromise.[^cite_note-\:17-660] A second scenario suggests the security establishment would sacrifice clerical rule, including Khamenei, for the sake of seizing power. Coercive measures would be used to prevent disorder and the new leadership "would present itself as the guardian of stability, national unity and territorial integrity," while replacing revolutionary ideology with pragmatism.[^cite_note-\:17-660] The third scenario is of simultaneous collapse of political authority and repressive forces, causing different branches of the IRGC and political factions to compete for control, possibly sparking civil war, with the potential of intervention by foreign forces.[^cite_note-\:17-660] The final scenario is the return of Pahlavi, who would assume power while the existing government collapses, senior clerics flee to Iraq, some security forces flee to Russia, and most of the IRGC is absorbed into the armed forces.[^cite_note-\:17-660]

CNN and the [Institute for the Study of War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_the_Study_of_War), suggested that groups such as the Kurdistan Freedom Party and the Kurdistan National Guard attacked government military bases and caused severe casualties.[^cite_note-KFParty-252][^cite_note-Sleiman-2026-22] A *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)* editorial on 2 February strongly encouraged Trump to carry out a military attack to help the protesters overthrow Iran's government, arguing it would be good for Iranians and US interests.[^cite_note-661]

Israeli foreign minister [Gideon Sa'ar](485357.md) said that only the Iranian people can topple the Iranian government.[^cite_note-662]

[^cite_note-114]: &#x20;Golkar, Saeid; Brodsky, Jason M. (5 January 2026). ["What's New About This Wave of Protests in Iran"](https://foreignpolicy.com/2026/01/05/iran-protests-islamic-republic-regime-change/). *Foreign Policy*. [Archived](https://ghostarchive.org/archive/20260226130216/https://foreignpolicy.com/2026/01/05/iran-protests-islamic-republic-regime-change/) from the original on 26 February 2026. Retrieved 9 January 2026. A notable development in late 2025 was the ideological shift within the protest slogans themselves. While 'Woman, Life, Freedom' remains symbolically powerful, new chants increasingly reflect monarchist sentiments. Calls such as 'Javid Shah' (Long Live the King) and 'This Is the Final Battle / Pahlavi Will Return' have echoed through the cities where the protests are concentrated. These slogans point to a renewed interest in the Pahlavi legacy and open calls for the return of Crown Prince Reza Pahlavi, representing a significant departure from the largely republican and rights-based framing of the 2022 movement. When combined with economic desperation, political exhaustion appears to be pushing parts of society toward alternative visions of order and stability.
